Some of the leading butchers of the metropolis are trying to mdse. scare about the milk supply. They insert that while there is great cry about tuberculoue meat, nothing is done to prevent the sale of tuberculou• milk. In other won., butchers who cell had meat are prosecuted, fined and impel.' seined, while dnirymon who sell impure milk are ' not even called to account. Of course the vendor. of meat ridicule the assertion of the secretary of the Tuberculosis Commission who recently declared at Leetle that it is only when the udder of the cow is di d that its milk is likely to convey infec- -1101,. They amen that this is wholly at vintner with the general practice of medical officers of health,who swear before the magistrates that tithe slightest appearance of tubercular deposit in appar- ent in part of a °arcane, the whole body most be denrroyed, as there is • danger to human life if it be used aa food. If this hat statement Ic true, not merely outchere, but the consumers of milk, have cause to demand that breeding from tuberculous cows, ehall be peremptorily stopped.
